\displaystyle\langle R_{s}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right),\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle\coloneqq r_{s}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)=\left(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},\qquad\langle R_{r}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle\coloneqq r_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)=\left(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

\displaystyle\langle L_{f}\left(p^{\mathrm{S}}\right),q^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle:=l_{f}\left(p^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}\right)=\left(p^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}},\qquad\langle L_{p}\left(p^{\mathrm{P}}\right),q^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle:=l_{p}\left(p^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\right)=\left(p^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

Lemma 5.9
.

The operators
R_{s},R_{r},L_{f}
, and
L_{p}
are bounded, continuous, and coercive.

Proof.

The operators satisfy the following continuity and coercivity bounds:

\displaystyle\langle R_{s}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right),\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\langle R_{s}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right),\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle\gtrsim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{X}_{d},

\displaystyle\langle R_{r}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\langle R_{r}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle\gtrsim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{r},

\displaystyle\langle L_{f}\left(p^{\mathrm{S}}\right),q^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\|q^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\langle L_{f}\left(p^{\mathrm{S}}\right),p^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle\gtrsim\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\qquad\forall p^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}\in W_{f},

\displaystyle\langle L_{p}\left(p^{\mathrm{P}}\right),q^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|q^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\langle L_{p}\left(p^{\mathrm{P}}\right),p^{\mathrm{P}}\rangle\gtrsim\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\qquad\forall p^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\in W_{p}.

The coercivity bounds follow directly from the definitions, using Körn’s inequality
[
17
]
for
R_{s}
, whereas the continuity bounds follow from Cauchy–Schwarz and Young’s inequalities.

For the regularization of the Lagrange multiplier, let
\psi(\lambda)\in{H}^{1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right)
be the solution of the auxiliary problem

\begin{gathered}-\nabla\cdot\nabla\psi(\lambda)=0\quad\text{ in }\quad\Omega_{\mathrm{P}},\\
\nabla\psi(\lambda)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}=\lambda\quad\text{ on }\Sigma,\quad\psi(\lambda)=0\quad\text{ on }\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}}.\end{gathered}

Note that the solution depends continuously on data, and the trace continuity imply that there exist positive constants
c^{\star}
and
C^{\star}
, such that

(5.21)

c^{\star}\|\psi(\lambda)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\leq\|\lambda\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}\leq C^{\star}\|\psi(\lambda)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

Lemma 5.10
.

The operator
L_{\lambda}:\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
, defined as

\langle L_{\lambda}\lambda,\xi\rangle=l_{\lambda}(\lambda,\xi):=(\nabla\psi(\lambda),\nabla\psi(\xi))_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

is continuous and coercive.

Proof.

It follows from (
5.21
) that there exist positive constants
c^{\star}
and
C^{\star}
such that

\langle L_{\lambda}\lambda,\xi\rangle\leq C^{\star}\|\lambda\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}\|\xi\|_{-1/2,\Sigma},\quad\langle L_{\lambda}\lambda,\lambda\rangle\geq c^{\star}\|\lambda\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2},\qquad\forall\lambda,\xi\in\Lambda.

In order to establish that
\mathbb{D}
is nonempty we first show that there exists a solution to a regularization of (
5.6a
)-(
5.6c
) and then send the regularization parameter to zero.

Lemma 5.11
.

The domain
\mathbb{D}
specified by (
5.5
) is nonempty.

Proof.

We follow four steps.

1. Regularization of (
5.7a
)-(
5.7b
):

For
\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}^{i}=(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},i},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},i},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S},i})\in\vec{\mathbf{V}},\vec{q}^{i}=\left(q^{\mathrm{P},i},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{i},q^{\mathrm{S},i},\mu^{i}\right)\in\vec{Q},i=1,2
, define the operators
\mathcal{R}:\vec{\mathbf{V}}\rightarrow\vec{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}:\vec{Q}\rightarrow\vec{Q}^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{R}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}^{1},\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle:=\langle R_{s}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},1}),\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},2}\rangle+\langle R_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},1}\right),\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},2}\rangle=r_{s}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},1},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},2}\right)+r_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},1},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},2}\right),

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}\vec{q}^{1},\vec{q}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle:=\langle L_{f}(q^{\mathrm{S},1}),q^{\mathrm{S},2}\rangle+\langle L_{p}(q^{\mathrm{P},1}),q^{\mathrm{P},2}\rangle+\langle L_{\Gamma}\left(\mu^{1}\right),\mu^{2}\rangle=l_{f}\left(q^{\mathrm{S},1},q^{\mathrm{S},2}\right)+l_{p}\left(q^{\mathrm{P},1},q^{\mathrm{P},2}\right)+l_{\Gamma}\left(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}\right).

For
\epsilon>0
, consider the regularized problem: given
\bar{f}\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime},\bar{g}\in\vec{Q}^{\prime}
, determine
\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}\in\vec{\mathbf{V}},\vec{q}_{\epsilon}\in\vec{Q}
satisfying

(5.22a)

\displaystyle(\epsilon\mathcal{R}+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{A})\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}+\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\vec{q}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bar{f}\quad\text{ in}\quad\vec{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime},

(5.22b)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{B}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}+\left(\epsilon\mathcal{L}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\right)\vec{q}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bar{g}\quad\text{ in}\quad\vec{Q}^{\prime}.

2. Existence of unique solution of (
5.22a
)-(
5.22b
):

Introduce the operator
\mathcal{O}:\vec{\mathbf{V}}\times\vec{Q}\rightarrow(\vec{\mathbf{V}}\times\vec{Q})^{\prime}
defined as

\mathcal{O}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\\
\vec{q}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\epsilon\mathcal{R}+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\\
-\mathcal{B}&\epsilon\mathcal{L}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\end{array}\right)\left[\begin{array}[]{l}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\\
\vec{q}\end{array}\right].

From Lemmas
5.1
-
5.2
it can be shown that
\mathcal{O}
is bounded and continuous. Additionally, leveraging Lemmas
5.1
and
5.10
, Assumptions in Section
4.1
, and the inequality

(5.23)

-(\xi a,a)-(\xi b,b)\leq 2(\xi a,b)\leq(\xi a,a)+(\xi b,b),

we can conclude that

\displaystyle\biggl\langle\mathcal{O}\begin{pmatrix}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\\
\vec{q}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\\
\vec{q}\end{pmatrix}\biggr\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon r_{s}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+\epsilon r_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}};\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle\quad-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})+\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}q^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{p}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\tau},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)+\epsilon l_{f}\left(q^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon l_{p}\left(q^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+\epsilon l_{\Gamma}(\mu,\mu)+\left(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\geq C\left(\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right.

(5.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{\tau}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|q^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+(1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}\|q^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\left.+\epsilon\|\mu\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right).

It follows that
\mathcal{O}
is coercive. Thus, an application of the Lax–Miligram Lemma establishes the existence of a solution
\left(\vec{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\epsilon},\vec{p}_{\epsilon}\right)\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}\times\vec{Q}
of (
5.22a
)-(
5.22b
), where
\vec{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\epsilon}=\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\right)
and
\vec{p}_{\epsilon}=\left(p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon},\lambda_{\epsilon}\right)
.

3. Uniform boundedness:

From inequality (
5.24
) and (
5.22a
)-(
5.22b
), we have that

\displaystyle\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+(1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Sigma)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right.

(5.25)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{g}_{p}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{g}_{e}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\right).

On the other hand, as a consequence of (
5.6b
), it follows that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{s,\epsilon}
satisfy

a_{p}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)-b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)=\left(A\bar{g}_{e},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{\tau}\in\mathbf{Z}.

Therefore, applying the inf-sup condition (
5.3a
), we obtain:

(5.26)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\left(0,\boldsymbol{\tau},0,0\right)\in\vec{Q}}\frac{b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)}{\|(0,\boldsymbol{\tau},0,0)\|_{\vec{Q}}}=\sup_{\left(0,\boldsymbol{\tau},0,0)\in\vec{Q}\right.}\frac{a_{p}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)-\left(A\bar{g}_{e},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}}{\|(0,\boldsymbol{\tau},0,0)\|_{\vec{Q}}}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|{\bar{g}}_{e}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

Combining (
5.25
) and (
5.26
), and using Young’s inequality,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+(1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|\bar{g}_{p}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{g}_{e}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right),

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|\bar{g}_{p}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{g}_{e}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}.

To obtain bounds for
p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}
, and
\lambda_{\epsilon}
we use (
5.3b
). With
\vec{p}=\left(p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon},0,p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon},\lambda_{\epsilon}\right)\in\vec{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\right)\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}}\frac{b^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon})+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p_{H,\epsilon})+b_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\mathbf{0};\lambda_{\epsilon})}{\|(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}})\|_{\vec{\mathbf{V}}}}

(5.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}.

Employing again Young’s inequality, (
5.27
), and (
5.28
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|^{2}_{-1/2,\Sigma}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\bar{g}_{p}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\bar{g}_{e}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right),

which implies that all the quantities
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
,
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}
,
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
,
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
,

\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
,
\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}
,
\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
, and
\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}
are bounded independently of
\epsilon
. Using (
5.22a
) and the continuity of
R_{r}
(cf. Lemma
5.9
), we can infer that

\displaystyle\left(\epsilon+1\right)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}},

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\epsilon}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\lambda_{\epsilon}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}+\|\bar{f}_{r}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\bar{f}_{s}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}.

Therefore
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
is also bounded independently of
\epsilon
.

4. Passing to the limit:

Since
\vec{\mathbf{V}}
and
\vec{Q}
are reflexive Banach spaces, as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
we can apply the Banach–Alaoglu–Bourbaki Theorem
[
18
]
to extract weakly convergent subsequences
\left\{\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon,n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
,
\left\{\vec{q}_{\epsilon,n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
\left\{\mathcal{A}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon,n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, such that
\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon,n}\rightarrow\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}
in
\vec{\mathbf{V}},\vec{q}_{\epsilon,n}\rightarrow\vec{q}
in
\vec{Q},\mathcal{A}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon,n}\rightarrow\zeta
in
\vec{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime}
, and

\zeta+\mathcal{E}_{1}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}+\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\vec{q}=\bar{f}\quad\text{in}\quad\vec{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime},\qquad\mathcal{E}_{2}\vec{q}-\mathcal{B}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}=\bar{g}\quad\text{in}\quad\vec{Q}^{\prime}.

Moreover, from (
5.22a
)–(
5.22b
) we can infer that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left(\langle\mathcal{A}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon},\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{E}_{1}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon},\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{E}_{2}\vec{q}_{\epsilon},\vec{q}_{\epsilon}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left(\bar{f}\left(\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}\right)+\bar{g}\left(\vec{q}_{\epsilon}\right)-\epsilon\langle\mathcal{R}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon},\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\epsilon}\rangle-\epsilon\langle\mathcal{L}\vec{q}_{\epsilon},\vec{q}_{\epsilon}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{f}(\vec{\boldsymbol{v}})+\bar{g}(\vec{q})=\zeta(\vec{\boldsymbol{v}})+\langle\mathcal{E}_{1}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}},\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{E}_{2}\vec{q},\vec{q}\rangle.

Since
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}
is monotone and continuous, it follows (see
[
40
, Def. on p. 38]
) that
\mathcal{A}\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}=\zeta
. Hence,
\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}
and
\vec{q}
solve (
5.6a
)-(
5.6c
), which establishes that
\mathbb{D}
is nonempty.

Corollary 5.12
.

Under the assumptions

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

,
we have that
\operatorname{Rg}(I+\mathcal{M})=\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We need to show that for
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
there exists
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{D}
such that
\boldsymbol{f}\in(I+\mathcal{M})(\boldsymbol{v})
.
Let
\left(\bar{f}_{r},\bar{f}_{s},\bar{g}_{p},\bar{g}_{e}\right)\in\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
. From Lemma
5.11
, there exists
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{e}\right)\in\mathbb{D}
solving (
5.6a
)-(
5.6c
). Hence
\left(\bar{f}_{r}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\bar{f}_{s}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\bar{g}_{p}-\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{e}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{e}\right)

\in\mathcal{M}\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{e}\right)
and therefore it follows that
\left(\bar{f}_{r},\bar{f}_{s},\bar{g}_{p},\bar{g}_{e}\right)\in(I+\mathcal{M})\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{e}\right)
.

Step 2:
Solvability of the parabolic problem.

We begin by showing that
\mathcal{M}
(cf. (
5.8
)) is a monotone operator.

Lemma 5.13
.

Under the assumptions

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

, the operator
\mathcal{M}
defined by (
5.1
) is monotone.

Proof.

We need to show for
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{M}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}})
that
(\boldsymbol{f}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{f}},\boldsymbol{v}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}})_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}\geq 0
.
For
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)\in\mathbb{D}
, it holds that
(\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma})\in\mathcal{M}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)
,
\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)\in\mathbb{S}_{2}
. And using the definition of inner product of
((\cdot,\cdot))_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}
, (
5.6a
) and (
5.6b
) we have

\displaystyle\left(\left(\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right),\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)\right)_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}};\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\phi\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}};\lambda\right)-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})

(5.29)

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})+b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)-b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\phi\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}\right)-b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)-\left(r_{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}\right)-\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\right).

Recalling the notation
\boldsymbol{v}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}),\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{e}),\boldsymbol{f}=(\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma}),\tilde{\boldsymbol{f}}=(\tilde{\bar{f}}_{r}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{\bar{f}}_{s}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\tilde{\bar{g}}_{p}-\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\bar{g}}_{e}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{e})
, we stress that the main objective is to prove, using (
5.29
), that

(\boldsymbol{f}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{f}},\boldsymbol{v}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}})\geq 0.

As the model problem is linear, demonstrating the coercivity of the bilinear forms provided in (
5.29
) is sufficient. We have already proven the coercivity of these bilinear forms in Lemmas
5.1
and
5.5
, and the continuity of
(r_{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}})
and
(\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}})
is straightforward. This implies that the operator
\mathcal{M}
is monotone.

Lemma 5.14
.

Assume that

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

hold. Then,
for each
\boldsymbol{h}_{r}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{W}_{r,2}^{\prime})
,
\boldsymbol{h}_{s}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{W}_{s,2}^{\prime})
,
h_{p}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W_{p,2}^{\prime})
and
h_{e}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{Z}_{2}^{\prime})
, and
\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{P}}_{r}(0)\in\mathbf{V}_{r},\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s}(0)\in\mathbf{X}_{d},p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\in W_{p},\boldsymbol{\sigma}(0)\in\mathbf{Z}
, there exists a solution to (
5.1
) with
\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r}),\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{s}\right),p^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;W_{p}\right)
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{Z}\right)
.

Proof.

Applying Theorem
5.7
with
\mathcal{N}=I,\mathcal{M}^{\star}=\mathcal{M},E=\mathbf{W}_{r,2}\times\mathbf{W}_{s,2}\times W_{p,2}\times\mathbf{Z}_{2}
,
E_{b}^{\prime}=\mathbf{W}_{r,2}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{W}_{s,2}^{\prime}\times W_{p,2}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{Z}_{2}^{\prime}
, and using Lemma
5.13
and Corollary
5.12
, we obtain existence of a solution to (
5.1
).

Step 3:
The mixed problem (
4.4
) is a special case of (
5.1
).

Finally, we establish the existence of a solution to (
4.4
) as a corollary of Lemma
5.14
.

Lemma 5.15
.

If
(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{P}}_{r}(t),\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s}(t),p^{\mathrm{P}}(t),\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(t))\in\mathbb{D}
solves (
5.1
) for
\boldsymbol{h}_{r}=\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{h}_{s}=\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},h_{p}=\rho_{f}^{-1}(1-\phi)^{-2}K\theta
and
h_{e}=0
, then it also solves (
4.4
).

Proof.

Let
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(t),\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(t),p^{\mathrm{P}}(t),\boldsymbol{\sigma}(t)\right)

\in\mathbb{D}
solve (
5.1
). For
\boldsymbol{h}_{r}=\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{h}_{s}=\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},h_{p}=\rho_{f}^{-1}(1-\phi)^{-2}K\theta
and
h_{e}=0
, there exist
\left(\bar{f}_{r},\bar{f}_{s},\bar{g}_{p},\bar{g}_{e}\right)\in\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\left(\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)\in\mathcal{M}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)

which satisfies

\frac{\mbox{d}}{\,\mbox{d}t}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
p^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\\
\rho_{f}^{-1}(1-\phi)^{-2}\kappa\theta\\
0\end{array}\right).

Then,

\left(\frac{\mbox{d}}{\,\mbox{d}t}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
p^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\end{array}\right),\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
q^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{\tau}\end{array}\right)\right)_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}+\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\end{array}\right),\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
q^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{\tau}\end{array}\right)\right)_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}=\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\\
\rho_{f}^{-1}(1-\phi)^{-2}K\theta\\
0\end{array}\right),\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\\
q^{\mathrm{P}}\\
\boldsymbol{\tau}\end{array}\right)\right)_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}.

From the definition of the inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}
, along with (
5.6a
) and (
5.6b
), we can deduce part of the first two equations in (
4.4
). Equation (
5.6c
), stemming directly from the definition of the domain
\mathbb{D}
, implies the remainder of (
4.4
).

Proof of Theorem
5.8
.

The existence of a solution of (
4.4
) follows from Lemma
5.14
and Lemma
5.15
. From Lemma
5.14
we have that
\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r}),\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{s}\right),p^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;W_{p}\right)
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{Z}\right)
. Next, by taking

(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau},\mu)=(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\lambda)
in (
4.4
), we obtain that
\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\in L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f}\right)
. Finally, the inf-sup condition (
5.3b
) and the first equation in (
4.4
) imply that
p^{\mathrm{S}}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{f})
and
\lambda\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\Lambda)
.

5.2
Existence and uniqueness of solution of the original formulation

In this section we discuss how the well-posedness of formulation (
4.1
) follows from the existence of a solution of the mixed formulation (
4.4
). First we recall that
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}
is the structure velocity, so the displacement solution can be recovered from

(5.30)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)=\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}+\int_{0}^{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,\quad\forall t\in(0,T].

Since
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{X}_{d})
, then
\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{X}_{d})
for any
\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}\in\mathbf{X}_{d}
. By construction,
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}
and
\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}
.

Theorem 5.16
.

Assume

(

⁢
H
.1

)

–

(

⁢
H
.3

)

. Then, for data
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f}^{\prime})
,
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))
,
r_{\mathrm{S}},\theta\in W^{1,1}(0,\\
T;W_{f}^{\prime})
, and initial conditions
\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{P}}_{r}(0)=\boldsymbol{u}_{r,0}\in\mathbf{V}_{r}
,
\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s}(0)=\boldsymbol{u}_{s,0}\in\mathbf{W}_{s}
,
p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=p^{\mathrm{P},0}\in W_{p}
, and
\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}\in\mathbf{V}_{s}
, there exists a unique solution
(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}

,\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\lambda)\in L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f}\right)\times L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{f})\times W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r})\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;W_{p}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{s})\times W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{W}_{s})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\Lambda)
of (
4.1
).

Proof.

We use the solvability of the mixed formulation (
4.4
) to establish the solvability of the original formulation (
4.1
). Let
(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\lambda)
be a solution to (
4.4
). Define
\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}
as in (
5.30
), so that
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}
. Then the second equation in (
4.4
), with
\boldsymbol{\tau}=\mathbf{0}
, implies the second equation in (
4.1
), and the third equation in (
4.4
) implies that in (
4.1
). Additionally, we note that the first equations in (
4.4
) and (
4.1
) differ only in their respective terms
a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)
and
b_{\text{sig}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)
.
Testing the second equation in (
4.4
) with
\boldsymbol{\tau}\in\mathbf{Z}
yields
\left(\partial_{t}\left(A\boldsymbol{\sigma}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)\right),\boldsymbol{\tau}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}=0
. Using the fact that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\mathbf{V}_{s}\right)\subseteq\mathbf{Z}
, this implies
\partial_{t}\left(A\boldsymbol{\sigma}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)\right)=\mathbf{0}
. Integrating from 0 to
t\in(0,T]
, and using
\boldsymbol{\sigma}(0)=A^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\right)
, we conclude that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}(t)=A^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\right)
.
Hence, we have

b_{\text{sig}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right)=\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}=\left(A^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}=a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right).

Therefore, the first equation in (
4.1
) implies that of (
4.4
), establishing that
\bigl(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}+\int_{0}^{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,\lambda\bigr)
is a solution of (
4.1
). From the weak formulation (
4.1
), it can ben seen that the structural velocity
\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}
is in
\mathbf{W}_{s}
.

Next, we provide a stability bound for the solution of (
4.1
).

Lemma 5.17
.

Assuming sufficient regularity of the data as well as

(

⁢
H
.1

)

–

(

⁢
H
.3

)

, there exists a positive constant

\hat{C}
(possibly depending on
K,\kappa,\rho_{f},\rho_{s},\lambda_{p},\mu_{f},\mu_{p},\phi,\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}},C_{K}
) such that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{BJS})}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})\right)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|\lambda\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\Lambda\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}\left(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\Omega))}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}^{2}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right),

where we introduced the notation
|\boldsymbol{v}|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{BJS})}:=\int_{0}^{t}|\boldsymbol{v}|_{\mathrm{BJS}}\,\mbox{d}s
.

Proof.

We test the system against
(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\lambda)
and add the first three equations.
Using the inequality (
5.23
) and we can derive the following estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\left(\left(\rho_{s}(1-\phi)\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(\sqrt{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}+\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\sqrt{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}+\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)+\left({(1-\phi)^{2}}{K}^{-1}p^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(2\mu_{p}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\left(2\mu_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\lambda_{p}\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}+\left({\phi^{2}}{\kappa}^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\quad+(2\mu_{f}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\left(2\mu_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(2\mu_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}-\left(2\mu_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

(5.31)

\displaystyle\leq\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\left(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(r_{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+(\rho_{f}^{-1}\theta,p^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

From now on we denote by
c_{1}=c_{1}\left(\rho_{f},\rho_{s},\phi,K,\alpha_{s},\alpha_{f},\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}\right)
a data-dependent constant used for lower bounds, and with
c_{2}=c_{2}\left(\rho_{f},\rho_{s},\phi,K,\lambda_{p},\mu_{f},\mu_{p},\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}\right)
one for upper bounds. From (
5.31
) and using Young’s inequality with
\epsilon>0
, we proceed to expand the right-hand side of that relation as

\displaystyle\texttt{RHS}_{\eqref{rhs}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\left(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\theta(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+c_{2}\epsilon_{1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right).

Let us denote now the left-hand side of (
5.31
) as
\texttt{LHS}_{\eqref{rhs}}
. Using

(

⁢
H
.1

)

–

(

⁢
H
.3

)

, Körn’s inequality, and the bound
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{\rho_{f}\phi}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}+\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{\rho_{f}\phi}^{2}
, we have

\texttt{LHS}_{\eqref{rhs}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(t)\|_{\rho_{s}(1-\phi)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{\rho_{f}\phi}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{\frac{(1-\phi)^{2}}{K}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\bigr)+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}+(\frac{\phi^{2}}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}.

Integrating
\texttt{LHS}_{\eqref{rhs}}
in time over
(0,t]
for arbitrary
t\in(0,T]
, and invoking Lemma
4.1
, yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\texttt{LHS}_{\eqref{rhs}}\geq

\displaystyle\left.\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(s)\|_{\rho_{s}(1-\phi)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(s)\|_{\rho_{f}\phi}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(s)\|^{2}_{\frac{(1-\phi)^{2}}{K}}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(s)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right)\right|_{s=0}^{s=t}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}\,\mbox{d}s+\alpha_{f}\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(s)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(s)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\,\mbox{d}s\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(s)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\,\mbox{d}s\right)-c_{2}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right).

Then we combine the lower and upper bounds together to obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\bigl(\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(s)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(s)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\bigr)\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}\epsilon_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\theta(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{c_{2}\epsilon_{1}}{c_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(t)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right).

Taking the supremum of
t
in
(0,T]
and using the upper bound
\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s)\,\mbox{d}s\leq T\|\varphi\|_{\infty}
we can readily get

\displaystyle(c_{1}-c_{2}T\epsilon_{1})(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+c_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+(c_{1}-c_{2}\epsilon_{1})\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})\right)}^{2}+c_{1}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\left(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}^{2}\right)+\frac{c_{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\left(\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}^{2}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}^{2}\right)

(5.32)

\displaystyle\quad+c_{2}\epsilon_{1}(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))})+c_{2}(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}).

Finally, we use the inf-sup condition (
5.3b
) for
\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{S}},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\lambda}
together with (
4.1
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|({p}^{\mathrm{S}},{p}^{\mathrm{P}},{\lambda})\|_{W_{f}\times W_{p}\times\Lambda}\lesssim\sup_{(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\in\mathbf{V}_{f}\times\mathbf{V}_{r}}\frac{b^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},{p}^{\mathrm{S}})+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},{p}_{\mathrm{P}})+b_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0};{\lambda})}{\|(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{\mathbf{V}_{f}\times\mathbf{V}_{r}}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sup_{(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\in\mathbf{V}_{f}\times\mathbf{V}_{r}}\left(\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}-a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}})-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-a_{\mathrm{BJS}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s};\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\mathbf{0})+m_{\theta}(\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})}{\|(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{\mathbf{V}_{f}\times\mathbf{V}_{r}}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\left.+\frac{m_{\theta}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}\left(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}\left(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+\left(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}}{\|(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{\mathbf{V}_{f}\times\mathbf{V}_{r}}}\right).

Using the continuity bounds available from Lemmas
5.1
and
5.2
, we have

\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{W_{f}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{W_{p}}+\|\lambda\|_{\Lambda}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

which in turn implies that

(5.33)

\epsilon_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\lambda\|_{\Lambda}^{2}\right)\leq\tilde{C}\epsilon_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right).

Adding (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) and choosing
\epsilon_{2}
and
\epsilon_{1}
small enough, readily yields the following bound

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})\right)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;{\mathbf{L}}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}^{2}\ +\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}^{2}+\|\lambda\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;{H}^{-1/2}(\Sigma)\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\Omega))}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}^{2}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2},

which completes the proof.

Corollary 5.18
.

Assume

(

⁢
H
.1

)

–

(

⁢
H
.3

)

. Then, there exists a unique
(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},\lambda)
in the product space
L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f}\right)\times L^{\infty}\left(0,T;W_{f}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r})\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;W_{p}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{X}_{d})\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{s}\right)\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\Lambda)
, solution of problem (
4.1
).

Proof.

Let
(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S},i},p^{\mathrm{S},i},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},i},p^{\mathrm{P},i},\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},i},\lambda^{i}),i=1,2
, be two solutions of (
4.1
) with the same data. Subtracting both weak formulations yields homogeneous initial data and zero right-hand side. Consider
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{f}=\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S},1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S},2},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{S}}=p^{\mathrm{S},1}-p^{\mathrm{S},2},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r}=\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},2},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}}=p^{\mathrm{P},1}-p^{\mathrm{P},2},\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s}=\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{1}-\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{2},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s}=\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},2},\tilde{\lambda}=\lambda^{1}-\lambda^{2}
, and substitute
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{S}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r},\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},\tilde{\lambda}\right)
instead of
\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{s},{p}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}},{p}_{\mathrm{P}},{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s},\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}},{\lambda}\right)
with homogeneous initial data and forcing terms in Lemma
5.17
. This readily gives

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}=\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{f}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})\right)}=\|\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}=\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}=0,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{r}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}=\|\tilde{p}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}=\|\tilde{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{H}^{-1/2}(\Sigma))}=0.

Hence, the solution to problem (
4.1
) must be unique.

6
Semi-discrete formulation

Suppose that
\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\mathrm{S}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\mathrm{P}}
are shape-regular quasi-uniform partitions of
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}
and
\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}
, respectively, both consisting of affine elements with maximal element diameter
h
. The two partitions may be non-matching at the interface
\Sigma
. For the discretization of the fluid velocity and pressure we choose conforming FE spaces
\mathbf{V}_{f,h}\subset\mathbf{V}_{f}
and

W_{f,h}\subset W_{f}
, which are assumed to be inf–sup stable (such as MINI elements, Taylor–Hood, and conforming Crouzeix–Raviart elements). For the discretization of the generalized Biot unknowns, we define
X_{h}^{k}=\left\{q\in C({\Omega}):\left.q\right|_{K}\in\mathbb{P}_{k}(K)\quad\forall K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}\right\}
where

\mathbb{P}_{k}(K)
denotes the space of polynomials of degree
k\geq 1
defined over
K
. With them, we define the following conforming discrete spaces:

\mathbf{V}_{r,h}=\mathbf{V}_{r}\cap\left[X_{h}^{k+1}\right]^{d},\quad\mathbf{V}_{s,h}=\mathbf{V}_{s}\cap\left[X_{h}^{k+1}\right]^{d},\quad W_{p,h}=W_{p}\cap X_{h}^{k},\quad\mathbf{W}_{s,h}=\mathbf{W}_{s}\cap\left[X_{h}^{k}\right]^{d}.

On
\mathbf{V}_{f,h},\mathbf{V}_{r,h}
and
\mathbf{V}_{s,h}
we prescribe
homogeneous boundary conditions on the external boundaries
\Gamma_{\mathrm{S}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}}
, and the discrete space for the Lagrange multiplier is

\Lambda_{h}=\mathbf{V}_{r,h}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}|_{\Sigma},

and it is equipped with the norm

\|\cdot\|_{\Lambda_{h}}=\|\cdot\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}
.

The first semi-discrete problem reads: find
(\boldsymbol{u}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\lambda_{h})\in L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f,h}\right)\times L^{\infty}\left(0,T;W_{f,h}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r,h}\right)

\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;W_{p,h}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{s,h}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{W}_{s,h}\right)\times L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\Lambda_{h}\right)
, such that

\displaystyle a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h};\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\lambda_{h}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{p}}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

(6.1a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

(6.1b)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{(1-\phi)^{2}}{K}\partial_{t}p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}-b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})-b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})-b^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h})=\left(r_{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\left(\rho_{f}^{-1}\theta,q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

(6.1c)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h};\mu_{h}\right)=0,

(6.1d)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ -m_{\rho_{p}}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{p}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})=0,

for all
\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\in\mathbf{V}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\in q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{r,h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\in q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{X}_{d,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{s,h}
and
\mu_{h}\in\Lambda_{h}
.
The initial conditions
\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}(0),\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}(0),p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}(0)
and
\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)
are suitable approximations of
\boldsymbol{v}^{\mathrm{P}}_{r,0},\boldsymbol{v}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s,0},

p^{\mathrm{P},0}
and
\boldsymbol{y}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s,0}
, respectively.
To prove that (
6.1a
)-(
6.1d
) is well-posed we follow the same strategy as in the continuous case.
Let
\mathbf{V}_{s,h}
consist of polynomials of degree at most
k_{s}
. The stress FE space
\mathbf{Z}_{h}\subset\mathbf{Z}
will have symmetric tensors with discontinuous polynomial entries of degree up to
k_{s-1}
:

\mathbf{Z}_{h}:=\left\{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\mathbf{Z}:\left.\boldsymbol{\sigma}\right|_{K}\in\mathbb{P}_{k_{s}-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}(K)^{d\times d},\ \forall K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right\}.

Then the second semi-discrete formulation is as before but we replace looking for
\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}
by seeking
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\in W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{Z}_{h}\right)
from the set of equations

\displaystyle a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\lambda_{h}\right)-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle\quad-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{p}}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

(6.2a)

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\right)=\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

\displaystyle\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}\partial_{t}p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}-b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)-b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)-b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)+a_{p}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\right)-b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\right)

(6.2b)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ =\left(q^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\left(\rho_{f}^{-1}\theta,q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

(6.2c)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\mu_{h}\right)=0,

for all
\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\in\mathbf{V}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\in W_{f,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{r,h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\in W_{p,h}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\in\mathbf{Z}_{h}
, and
\mu_{h}\in\Lambda_{h}
.
We demonstrate the well-posedness of the mixed formulation and subsequently deduce the well-posedness of the primal formulation, akin to the continuous case.
The initial conditions
\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}(0),\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}(0),p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}(0)
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}(0)
are suitable approximations of
\boldsymbol{v}^{\mathrm{P}}_{r,0},\boldsymbol{v}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s,0},p^{\mathrm{P},0}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{0}=A^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\left(\boldsymbol{y}^{\mathrm{P}}_{s,0}\right)
.
We group the spaces and test functions as:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\coloneqq\mathbf{V}_{r,h}\times\mathbf{V}_{s,h}\times\mathbf{V}_{f,h},&\vec{Q}_{h}\coloneqq W_{p,h}\times\mathbf{Z}_{h}\times W_{f,h}\times\Lambda_{h},\\
\vec{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}\coloneqq(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h})\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h},&\vec{p}_{h}\coloneqq(p_{p,h},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\lambda_{h})\in\vec{Q}_{h},\\
\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{h}\coloneqq(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}})\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h},&\vec{q}_{h}:=(q_{p,h},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\mu_{h})\in\vec{Q}_{h},\end{array}

where the spaces
\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
and
\vec{Q}_{h}
are endowed with the same norms as in the continuous spaces.

6.1
Discrete inf-sup conditions

Lemma 6.1
.

There exist constants
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}>0
, independent of
h
such that

(6.3a)

\displaystyle\inf_{\left(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0}\right)\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\sup_{\left(0,\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h},0,0\right)\in\vec{Q}_{h}}\frac{b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\right)}{\|(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0})\|_{\vec{\mathbf{V}}}\|(0,\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h},0,0)\|_{\vec{Q}}}

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{1},

(6.3b)

\displaystyle\inf_{\left(q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\mathbf{0},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\mu_{h}\right)\in\vec{Q}_{h}}\sup_{\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\right)\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\mathbf{0};\mu_{h}\right)}{\|(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}})\|_{\vec{\mathbf{V}}}\|(q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\mathbf{0},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\lambda_{h})\|_{\vec{Q}}}

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{2}.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{0}\neq(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n},\mathbf{0})\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
be given. We choose
\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}^{n}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n})
and, using Körn’s inequality
[
17
]
, we obtain

\frac{b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}^{n})}{\|\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}}=\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\geq\beta_{1}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

Therefore, (
6.3a
) holds.

Equation (
6.3b
) follows from the inf-sup condition for the Stokes problem
[
35
,
15
]
and the weighted inf-sup condition in
[
9
, Theorem 7]
. The proof of the inf-sup condition for
b_{\Gamma}(\cdot,\cdot;\cdot)

can be done similarly to

[
34
, Corollary 3.5]
.

Corollary 6.2
.

There exists a constant
\beta_{3}>0
, independent of
h
such that

\inf_{\vec{q_{h}}\in\vec{Q}}\sup_{\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{h}\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}}\frac{\langle\mathcal{B}(\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{h}),\vec{q}_{h}\rangle}{\|\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}_{h}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{V}}}\|\vec{q}_{h}\|_{\vec{Q}}}\geq\beta_{3}.

Proof.

The statement follows from Lemma
5.5
by simply taking
\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}=\boldsymbol{0}
.

6.2
Existence and uniqueness of solution

In order to show well-posedness of (
6.2a
)-(
6.2c
), we proceed as in the case of the continuous problem. We introduce
\mathbf{W}_{r,h}^{2}
,
\mathbf{W}_{s,h}^{2}

W_{p,h}^{2}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{h}^{2}
as the closure of the spaces
\mathbf{V}_{r,h}
,
\mathbf{V}_{s,h}
,
W_{p,h}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{h}
with respect to the norms (
5.4
), and denote

\mathbb{S}_{2,h}=\mathbf{W}_{r,h}^{2}\times\mathbf{W}_{s,h}^{2}\times W_{p,h}^{2}\times\mathbf{Z}_{h}^{2}
, and define the discrete domain

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{h}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\right)\in\mathbf{V}_{r,h}\times\mathbf{X}_{d,h}\times W_{p,h}\times\mathbf{Z}_{h}:\text{ for given }\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},r_{\mathrm{S}}\right)\in\left(\mathbf{V}_{f}^{\prime},W_{f}^{\prime}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\exists\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\lambda_{h}\right)\in\mathbf{V}_{f,h}\times W_{f,h}\times\Lambda_{h}\text{ such that }\forall\left(\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\right),\left(p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\mu_{h}\right)\right)

(6.4)

\displaystyle\in\vec{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\times\vec{Q}_{h}\text{ satisfy }\eqref{DD1}-\eqref{DD3}\left.\text{ for some }\left(\bar{f}_{r},\bar{f}_{s},\bar{g}_{p},\bar{g}_{e}\right)\in\mathbb{S}_{2,h}^{\prime}\right\}.

The equations defining the domain are

\displaystyle a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)+b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\lambda_{h}\right)-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle\quad-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{p}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

(6.5a)

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\right)=\left(\rho_{p}\bar{f}_{s},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(\rho_{f}\phi\bar{f}_{r},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}},

\displaystyle\left(\frac{(1-\phi)^{2}}{K}p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}-b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)-b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\phi\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)-b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)+a_{p}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\right)-b_{\text{sig}}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\right)

(6.5b)

\displaystyle\quad=\left(q^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}\bar{g}_{p},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\left(A\bar{g}_{e},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

(6.5c)

\displaystyle b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\mu_{h}\right)=0.

Analogously to the continuous formulation, we define
\mathcal{M}_{h}
with domain
\mathbb{D}_{h}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{h}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\right)\right):=

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\bar{f}_{r}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{f}_{s}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\bar{g}_{p}-p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\bar{g}_{e}-\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\right)\in\mathbb{S}_{2,h}^{\prime}:\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}\right)\text{ solves }\eqref{DD1}-\eqref{DD3}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\text{ for }\left(\bar{f}_{r},\bar{f}_{s},\bar{g}_{p},\bar{g}_{e}\right)\in\mathbb{S}_{2,h}^{\prime}\right\},

and consider the problem

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{d}}{\,\mbox{d}t}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\\
\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\\
p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}(t)\\
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}(t)\end{array}\right)+\mathcal{M}_{h}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\\
\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(t)\\
p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}(t)\\
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}(t)\end{array}\right)\ni\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\\
\rho_{f}^{-1}(1-\phi)^{-2}K\theta\\
\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right).

Theorem 6.3
.

For each
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f}^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r}^{\prime}),r_{\mathrm{S}}\in W^{1,1}(0,T;W_{f}^{\prime})
,
\theta\in W^{1,1}\left(0,T;W_{p}^{\prime}\right)
, and
p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}(0)\in W_{p,h},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h}(0)=A^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0))\in\mathbf{Z}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\in\mathbf{V}_{r,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\in\mathbf{V}_{s,h}
under assumptions

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

, there exists a solution of (
6.2a
)-(
6.2b
) with
(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}
,
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{h},\lambda_{h})\in L^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{f,h}\right)\times L^{\infty}\left(0,T;W_{f,h}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{r,h}\right)\times

W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;W_{p,h}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{V}_{s,h}\right)\times W^{1,\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{Z}_{h}\right)\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\Lambda_{h})
.

To prove Theorem
6.3
we proceed as in the continuous problem:
first we establish that
\mathbb{D}_{h}
is nonempty, secondly we
show solvability of the parabolic problem (
6.2
), and then show that the solution to (
6.4
) satisfies (
6.2a
)-(
6.2c
).
With the established discrete inf-sup conditions (
6.3a
) and (
6.3b
), the proof follows closely the proof of Theorem
5.8
. The only difference is that the operator
L_{\Gamma}
from Lemma
5.10
is now defined as
L_{\Gamma}:\Lambda_{h}\rightarrow\Lambda_{h}^{\prime},\langle L_{\Gamma}\mu_{h,1},\mu_{h,2}\rangle:=\langle\mu_{h,1},\mu_{h,2}\rangle_{-\frac{1}{2},h,\Gamma_{h}}
. One needs to establish that
L_{\Gamma}
is a bounded, continuous, coercive and monotone operator, which follows immediately from its definition, since
\langle L_{\Gamma}\mu_{h},\mu_{h}\rangle^{1/2}=\|\mu_{h}\|_{-\frac{1}{2},\Lambda_{h}}
.

As a corollary of Theorem
6.3
, we obtain the following well-posedness result for the original semidiscrete problem (
6.1a
)-(
6.1d
). The proof is identical to the proof of Theorem
5.16
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Under the same assumptions as Theorem
6.3
, there exists a unique solution
(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h},p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},
