) with
\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{I}_{r,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}
,
\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{I}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}
,
p_{h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=Q_{r,h}p_{p,0}
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{Q}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,0}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}-p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})})}+\left|\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)-\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)\right|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{BJS})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}-p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}-p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\lambda-\lambda_{h}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1/2}(\Sigma))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\exp(T)}\bigg[h^{r_{k_{f}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{f}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}\right))}+h^{r_{s_{f}}+1}\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{r_{s_{f}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}\right))}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{k_{p}}-1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;H^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right)\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{k_{s}}-1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}\right)+h^{r_{s_{s}}+1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{s_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{s_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+h^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}+\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|\lambda\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}}\left(\Sigma\right))}+\|\lambda\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{H}^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}}\left(\Sigma\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\lambda\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{H}^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}}\left(\Sigma\right))}\right)\bigg],

where
0\leq r_{k_{f}}\leq k_{f}
,
0\leq r_{s_{f}}\leq s_{f}
,
1\leq r_{k_{p}}\leq k_{p}
,
0\leq r_{s_{p}}\leq s_{p}
,

1\leq r_{k_{s}}\leq k_{s}
,
0\leq r_{s_{s}}\leq s_{s}
,
-1/2\leq{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}\leq\tilde{k}_{p}-1/2
.

Remark 6.1
.

We observe that both relative velocity and solid displacement exhibit sub-optimal convergence. The solid and relative velocity blocks in the diagonal of the system matrix makes it difficult to derive a bound in the energy norm.

7
Fully discrete formulation

7.1
Definition and unique solvability

For the time discretization we employ the backward Euler method with constant time-step
\tau
,
T=N\tau
, and let
t_{n}=n\tau
,
0\leq n\leq N
. Let
d_{\tau}u^{n}:=\tau^{-1}(u^{n}-u^{n-1})
be the
first order (backward) discrete time derivative, where
u^{n}\approx u(t_{n})
. The fully discrete problem reads: given
\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0),\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{0}=\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0),\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{0}=\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0),\text{ and }p^{\mathrm{P},0}_{h}={p}_{h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)
, find
\boldsymbol{u}_{f,h}^{S,n}\in\mathbf{V}_{f,h},p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h}\in W_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\in\mathbf{V}_{r,h},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\in W_{r,h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\in\mathbf{V}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\in\mathbf{W}_{s,h},\lambda_{h}^{n}\in\Lambda_{h},
such that for
1\leq n\leq N
, there holds

\displaystyle a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle\quad+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n};\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+b^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h})+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h})+b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},(p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})^{n})+b_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}};\lambda_{h}^{n})

\displaystyle\quad-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})-m_{\theta}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{p}}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})

(7.1a)

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}^{n},\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}^{n},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}^{n},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

(7.1b)

\displaystyle((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}d_{\tau}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}-b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})-b_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})-b^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h})=(r_{\mathrm{S}}^{n},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+(\rho_{f}^{-1}\theta^{n},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

(7.1c)

\displaystyle b_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n};\mu_{h})=0,

(7.1d)

\displaystyle-m_{\rho_{p}}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})+m_{\rho_{p}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})=0,

for all
\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}}\in\mathbf{V}_{f,h},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\in W_{f,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{r,h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\in W_{r,h},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{V}_{s,h},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathbf{W}_{s,h},\mu_{h}\in\Lambda_{h}
.
The method requires solving at each time step the algebraic system

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\mathbf{X}=\tilde{\mathcal{F}},

where the matrix
\mathcal{L}
is the sum of the matrices
\mathbf{E}
(
4.2
) and
\mathbf{H}
(
4.2
), with
\mathbf{E}
scaled by
\frac{1}{\tau}
. The tilde notation on the right-hand side vectors signifies that they include contributions from the backward Euler time discretization.

Theorem 7.1
.

The fully discrete method (
7.2
) has a unique solution under the assumptions

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

.

Proof.

The aim is to show that the matrix
\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbf{E}+{\mathbf{H}}
is invertible.
Now, we proceed directly from the bilinear forms testing with
(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},\frac{1}{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\lambda_{h}^{n})
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbf{E}+{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle=a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h})+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}a_{\mathrm{BJS}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n};\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\rho_{p}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\qquad

\displaystyle=a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h})+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}a_{\mathrm{BJS}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n};\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\rho_{p}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}).

We bound some of the terms above using the inequality (
5.23
). This gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})\geq-\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\right)-\frac{1}{\tau}a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})-\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\frac{1}{\tau}m_{\rho_{p}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}).

And by combining both estimates above, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbf{E}+{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle\quad\gtrsim\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}-\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}|^{2}_{\mathrm{BJS}}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

It is clear that all coefficients that form the terms on the right-hand side are positive. Hence, the matrix on the left-hand side is coercive and consequently the matrix obtained from the system (
7.2
) is non-singular. The proof of uniqueness follows from the semi-discrete case since the system (
7.2
) is a scaled matrix.

7.2
Stability analysis of the fully discrete scheme

In this lemma, we discuss the stability analysis of fully discrete problem.
We will make use of the discrete space–time
norms

\|\phi\|_{l^{2}(0,T;X)}^{2}:=\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\phi^{n}\|_{X}^{2},\quad\quad\|\phi\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;X)}^{2}:=\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|\phi^{n}\|_{X}^{2},\quad\quad\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}\right|_{l^{2}(0,T;{\mathrm{BJS}})}=\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}\right|^{2}_{\mathrm{BJS}}.

Lemma 7.2
.

Under assumptions

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

, the fully discrete solution to (
7.1a
)-(
7.1d
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\left|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\right|_{l^{2}(0,T;{\mathrm{BJS}})}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h}\|_{l^{2}\left(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}\left(0,T;{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\|_{l^{2}\left(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})\right)}+\|\lambda_{h}\|_{l^{2}\left(0,T;\Lambda_{h}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}\left(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|\theta\|_{l^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right),

where

\hat{C}(K,\kappa,\rho_{f},\rho_{s},\lambda_{p},\mu_{f},\mu_{p},\phi,\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}},C_{T},C_{I},C_{K})
is a positive constant.

Proof.

We choose
(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\mu_{h})=(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\lambda_{h}^{n})
in (
7.1a
)-(
7.1d
) and using the (
5.23
), we have

\displaystyle a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},\boldsymbol{u}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S},n})+a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}d_{\tau}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+m_{\rho_{s}(1-\phi)}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}\left(d_{\tau}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}),(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})\right)+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n};\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(r_{\mathrm{S}}^{n},p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+(\rho_{f}^{-1}\theta^{n},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

Now, as a consequence of the following identity

(7.3)

\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}{\Upsilon}^{n}d_{\tau}\Upsilon^{n}=\frac{1}{2}d_{\tau}\|\Upsilon^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\frac{1}{2}\tau\|d_{\tau}\Upsilon^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

we readily obtain the energy inequality

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\tau}}{2}\bigl(\rho_{s}(1-\phi)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+{(1-\phi)^{2}}{K}^{-1}\|p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+2\mu_{p}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\lambda_{p}\|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\rho_{f}\phi\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\tau}{2}\left(\rho_{s}(1-\phi)\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(1-\phi)^{2}{K}^{-1}\|d_{\tau}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+2\mu_{p}\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\lambda_{p}\|d_{\tau}\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\rho_{f}\phi\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right)+\left|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}+\left({\phi^{2}}{\kappa}^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n},\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+2\mu_{f}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}),\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}))_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}

\displaystyle\leq\langle\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n}),\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\rangle_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+(\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t_{n}),\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t_{n}),\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+(r_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n}),p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}+(\rho_{f}^{-1}\theta(t_{n}),p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h})_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}.

Using Cauchy–Schwarz and Young’s inequalities along with Lemma
5.1
, noting that
\tilde{C}
depends on the parameters, and using
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
, we can sum over
n=1,\ldots,N
and multiply by
\tau
, to obtain

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},N}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},N}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},N}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\left|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{\tau}^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\bigg(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},0}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+{\epsilon_{1}}^{-1}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n})\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\theta(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

(7.4)

\displaystyle\qquad+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2})+\epsilon_{1}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2})\bigg).

Next, employing the inf–sup condition for
(p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h},p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h},\lambda_{h}^{n})
, in a similar way, we readily obtain

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigl(\|p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h}\|_{W_{f,h}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|_{W_{p,h}}^{2}+\|\lambda_{h}^{n}\|_{\Lambda_{h}}^{2}\bigr)

(7.5)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tilde{C}\epsilon_{2}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\bigr).

Combining (
7.4
) and (
7.5
), and taking
\epsilon_{2}
small enough, and then
\epsilon_{1}
small enough, we can assert that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},N}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},N}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},N}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\left|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{\tau}^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\|p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h}\|_{W_{f,h}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}\|_{W_{p,h}}^{2}+\|\lambda_{h}^{n}\|_{\Lambda_{h}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n})\|_{-1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\theta(t_{n})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{\mathrm{S}}(t_{n})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})}^{2})+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|p^{\mathrm{P},0}_{h}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2},

which completes the proof.

Lemma 7.3
.

(Discrete Gröwnwall inequality
[
36
]
)
Let
\tau>0,B\geq 0,
and let
a_{n},b_{n},c_{n},d_{n}\geq 0
be non-negative sequences such that
a_{0}\leq B
and

a_{n}+\tau\sum_{l=1}^{n}b_{l}\leq\tau\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}d_{l}a_{l}+\tau\sum_{l=1}^{n}c_{l}+B,\qquad n\geq 1.

Then, there holds

(7.6)

\displaystyle a_{n}+\tau\sum_{l=1}^{n}b_{l}\leq\exp\left(\tau\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}d_{l}\right)\left(\tau\sum_{l=1}^{n}c_{l}+B\right),\qquad n\geq 1.

7.3
Error estimates for the fully discrete scheme

Theorem 7.4
.

Assuming

(

⁢
H
.1

)

-

(

⁢
H
.3

)

and sufficient smoothness for the solution of (
4.1
), the solution of
(
6.1a
)-(
6.1d
) with
\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{I}_{r,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,0}
,
\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{I}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}
,
p_{P,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=Q_{r,h}p_{p,0}
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{Q}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,0}
, satisfies

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}-p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})})}+|(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})-(\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S}}_{f,h}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}})|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{BJS})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|p^{\mathrm{S}}-p^{\mathrm{S}}_{h}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}-p^{\mathrm{P}}_{h}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\lambda-\lambda_{h}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;H^{-1/2}(\Sigma))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\exp(T)}\biggl[h^{r_{k_{f}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{f}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}\right))}+h^{r_{s_{s}}+1}(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{s_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{s_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))})

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{k_{p}}-1}(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))})

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{s_{p}}+1}(\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;H^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;H^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;H^{r_{s_{p}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right)\right)})

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{k_{s}}-1}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}+\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{r_{k_{s}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}\right))}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+h^{r_{s_{f}}+1}\|p^{\mathrm{S}}\|_{l^{2}(0,T;H^{r_{s_{f}}+1}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}\right))}+h^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}+\frac{1}{2}}(\|\lambda\|_{l^{2}(0,T;H^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}}\left(\Sigma\right))}+\|\lambda\|_{l^{\infty}(0,T;H^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}}\left(\Sigma\right))}+\|\partial_{t}\lambda\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}}\left(\Sigma\right))})\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+\tau\biggl[\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\partial_{ttt}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}+\|\partial_{tt}p^{\mathrm{P}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}))}\biggr],

where
0\leq r_{k_{f}}\leq k_{f}
,
0\leq r_{s_{f}}\leq s_{f}
,
1\leq r_{k_{p}}\leq k_{p}
,
0\leq r_{s_{p}}\leq s_{p}
,

1\leq r_{k_{s}}\leq k_{s}
,
0\leq r_{s_{s}}\leq s_{s}
,
-1/2\leq{r}_{\tilde{k}_{p}}\leq\tilde{k}_{p}-1/2
.

Proof.

We introduce the errors for all variables and split them into approximation and discretization errors:

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{f}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{{\mathrm{S}},n}-\boldsymbol{u}_{f,h}^{{\mathrm{S}},n}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{{\mathrm{S}},n}-\mathbf{I}_{f,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{{\mathrm{S}},n})+(\mathbf{I}_{f,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{{\mathrm{S}},n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\mathrm{S},n}_{f,h}):=\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{r}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},n}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},n}-\mathbf{I}_{r,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},n})+(\mathbf{I}_{r,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P},n}-\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}):=\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{s}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{{\mathrm{P}},n}-\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{{\mathrm{P}},n}-\mathbf{I}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{{\mathrm{P}},n})+(\mathbf{I}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{{\mathrm{P}},n}-\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}):=\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{ss}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},n}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},n}-\mathbf{Q}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},n})+(\mathbf{Q}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P},n}-\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P},n}):=\boldsymbol{\chi}_{ss}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n},

\displaystyle e_{fp}^{n}

\displaystyle:=p^{\mathrm{S},n}-p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h}=(p^{\mathrm{S},n}-Q_{f,h}p^{\mathrm{S},n})+(Q_{f,h}p^{\mathrm{S},n}-p^{\mathrm{S},n}_{h}):=\chi_{fp}^{n}+\phi_{fp,h}^{n},

\displaystyle e_{pp}^{n}

\displaystyle:=p^{\mathrm{P},n}-p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}=(p^{\mathrm{P},n}-Q_{p,h}p^{\mathrm{P},n})+(Q_{p,h}p^{\mathrm{P},n}-p^{\mathrm{P},n}_{h}):=\chi_{pp}^{n}+\phi_{pp,h}^{n},

\displaystyle e_{\lambda}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\lambda^{n}-\lambda_{h}^{n}=(\lambda^{n}-Q_{\lambda,h}\lambda^{n})+(Q_{\lambda,h}\lambda^{n}-\lambda_{h}^{n}):=\chi_{\lambda}^{n}+\phi_{\lambda,h}^{n}.

The error equations are obtained by subtracting the first two equations of (
7.1a
)-(
7.1b
) from the their continuous counterpart (
4.1
).
Let
r_{n}
denote the difference between the time derivative and its discrete analogue
r_{n}(\theta)=\partial_{t}\theta(t_{n})-d_{\tau}\theta^{n}
.

The proof follows the structure of the proof of Theorem
6.7
:

(i)

Substitute
(\boldsymbol{v}_{f,h}^{\mathrm{S}},\boldsymbol{v}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{w}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},\boldsymbol{v}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}},q^{\mathrm{S}}_{h},q^{\mathrm{P}}_{h})=(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n},\phi_{fp,h}^{n},\phi_{pp,h}^{n})
in the error equation; and

(ii)

Split the individual errors
and apply the properties of the projection operators.

This gives

\displaystyle a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\right)+a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)+a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n};\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})+m_{\rho_{p}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})

\displaystyle\quad+m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})+\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}d_{\tau}\phi_{pp,h}^{n},\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})-m_{\theta}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})

\displaystyle\quad-m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})-m_{\theta}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})+m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})

(7.7)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}+\mathcal{H},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle:=-a_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\right)-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\right)-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\right)-a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n};\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)-b^{\mathrm{S}}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},\chi_{fp}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle-b_{\Gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n},\mathbf{0};\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\right)+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n},\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\right)+m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})+m_{\theta}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})+m_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})+m_{\theta}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})

\displaystyle-m_{\phi^{2}/\kappa}(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{p}}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{ss}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{ss}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})

\displaystyle-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\right)+m_{\theta}(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})+m_{\theta}(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{p}}(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n})

\displaystyle-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})-m_{\rho_{f}\phi}(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n})+b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\right)-\left((1-\phi)^{2}K^{-1}r_{n}(p),\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\right)_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle:=-a_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)-b_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n},\chi_{pp}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle-a_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}),d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)-a_{\mathrm{BJS}}\left(\mathbf{0},r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}});\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)-b_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n};\chi_{\lambda}^{n}).

By using the inequality (
5.23
) and the identity (
7.3
), the left-hand side of (
7.7
) becomes

\displaystyle\texttt{LHS}_{\eqref{fe1}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{\tau}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)+\frac{\tau}{2}\left(\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\|d_{\tau}\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)+\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}.

Now, we bound the terms on the right-hand side similarly as in the proof of Theorem
6.7
. Consequently we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{1}^{-1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{ss}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{fp}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\|\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(p^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right)+C\|\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2}

(7.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right).

Combining (
7.7
) and (
7.8
), summing over
n=1,\ldots,N
, and multiplying by
\tau
, we use Lemma
5.1
to obtain:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{N}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\phi_{pp,h}^{N}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{N}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+{\tau}^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl[\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\phi_{pp,h}^{0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{0}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\epsilon_{1}^{-1}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{ss}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{fp}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|r_{n}(p^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right)+\epsilon_{1}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2})+C\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\|\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2}+\mathcal{H})\biggr].

Next, for each term in
\mathcal{H}
, we use the following summation by parts

\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\upsilon\left(t_{n}\right),d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)=\left(\upsilon\left(t_{N}\right),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{N}\right)-\left(\upsilon(0),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{0}\right)-\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\left(d_{\tau}\upsilon^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right),

where
\upsilon
stands for any of the functions in
\mathcal{H}
.
Then, we apply Cauchy–Schwarz and Young’s inequalities to obtain

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\upsilon(t_{n}),d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{N}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\epsilon_{1}}\|\upsilon(t_{N})\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{0}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\upsilon(0)\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\|d_{\tau}\upsilon^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\bigr).

Next we proceed to bound each term of
\mathcal{H}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle\leq{\epsilon_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+{\tau}\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-1}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{N}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{pp}^{N}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|r_{N}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\chi_{\lambda}^{N}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left(\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{0}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{pp}^{0}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|r_{0}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\chi_{\lambda}^{0}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{P}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\left(\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\chi_{pp}^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|d_{\tau}r_{n}(\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}})\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|d_{\tau}\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\|_{-1/2,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right).

For the initial conditions, we set
\boldsymbol{u}_{r,h}^{\mathrm{P}}=\mathbf{I}_{r,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{r,0}
,
\boldsymbol{u}_{s,h}=\mathbf{Q}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{u}_{s,0}
,
p_{h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=Q_{p,h}p_{p,0}
, and
\boldsymbol{y}_{s,h}^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=\mathbf{I}_{s,h}\boldsymbol{y}_{s,0}
, implying

\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{0}=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ss,h}^{0}=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{0}=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\phi_{pp,h}^{0}=0.

Analogously to equation (
6.41
), we have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigl(\|\phi_{fp,h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}})}^{2}+\|\phi_{pp,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\phi_{\lambda,h}^{n}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2}\bigr)\lesssim\epsilon_{2}\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+h^{-2}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{f,h}^{n}-d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{n}|_{\mathrm{BJS}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{r,h}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{fp}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{pp}^{n}\|_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\|_{-1/2,\Sigma}^{2}\bigr).

We note that the terms involving
d_{\tau}
on the right-hand side require special treatment similarly as in
[
38
]
. Consequently

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\left(\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|d_{\tau}\chi_{pp}^{n}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|d_{\tau}\chi_{\lambda}^{n}\|_{-1/2,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t_{N}}\left(\|\partial_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{r}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{\tau}\chi_{pp}\|^{2}_{0,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}+\|\partial_{\tau}\chi_{\lambda}\|_{-1/2,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\right).

To bound
\|d_{\tau}d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
, we use the Integral Mean Value and Mean Value Theorems. Therefore

\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\|d_{\tau}d_{\tau}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}^{n}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}\leq C\underset{t\in\left(0,t_{N}\right)}{\operatorname{esssup}}\|\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{s}\|_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}^{2}.

On the other hand, regarding the time discretization error, Taylor’s expansion gives

\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|r_{n}(\phi)\|_{H^{k}(S)}^{2}\leq C\tau^{2}\|\partial_{tt}\phi\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;H^{k}(S)\right)}^{2},

and similarly for the higher-order derivatives, we obtain

(7.10)

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|d_{\tau}r_{n}(\phi)\|_{H^{k}(S)}^{2}\leq C\tau^{2}\|\partial_{ttt}\phi\|_{L^{2}\left(0,T;H^{k}(S)\right)}^{2}.

The assertion of the theorem follows from combining (
7.9
)-(
7.10
), the discrete Grönwall inequality (
7.6
) for
a_{n}=|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{s,h}^{N}|^{2}_{1,\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}
, triangle inequality, and the approximation properties (
6.19a
) (resp. (
6.30a
)) applied to (
6.19c
), (
6.20
), (resp. (
6.30c
)).

8
Numerical experiments

All the routines have been implemented using the open-source finite element library FEniCS
[
1
]
, along with the specialized module multiphenics
[
8
]
. This module was used to handle specific terms related to subdomains and boundaries. The solvers used in this work are monolithic. We utilized the MUMPS
[
6
]
distributed direct solver for the linear systems in the first three examples and employed UMFPACK
[
27
]
for the fourth example. We showcase four examples: convergence tests (example 1), simulation of subsurface fracture flow (example 2), extensible channel flow (example 3), and flow and deformation patterns in 2D slices of the brain (example 4).

8.1
Convergence tests against manufactured solutions

The accuracy of the spatio-temporal discretization is verified using the following closed-form solutions defined on the domains
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}=(0,1)\times(0,1),\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}=(0,1)\times(1,2)
, separated by the interface
\Sigma=(0,1)\times\{1\}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}=\sin(t)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\cos(\pi x)\sin(\pi y)\\
\sin(\pi x)\cos(\pi y)\end{array}\right),\qquad p^{\mathrm{S}}=\sin(t)\cos(\pi x)\cos(\pi y),\qquad\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}t^{2}\sin^{2}(4\pi y)-tx^{3}\cos(4\pi y)\\
t^{2}\sin^{2}(4\pi y)+2tx^{3}\sin(4\pi y)\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}tx^{3}\cos(4\pi y)\\
-2tx^{3}\sin(4\pi y)\end{array}\right),\qquad\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0.5t^{2}x^{3}\cos(4\pi y)\\
-t^{2}x^{3}\sin(4\pi y)\end{array}\right),\qquad p^{\mathrm{P}}=\cos(t)\sin(\pi x)\sin(\pi y).

The synthetic model parameters are taken as

\lambda_{p}=10,\quad\mu_{p}=10,\quad\mu_{f}=10,\quad\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}=1,\quad\phi=0.1,\quad\kappa=1,\quad\rho_{p}=1,\quad\rho_{f}=1,\quad K=1,\quad\theta=-0.01,

all regarded non-dimensional and do not have physical relevance in this case, as we will be simply testing the convergence of the FE approximations.
The model problem is then complemented with the appropriate Dirichlet boundary conditions and initial data. These functions do not necessarily fulfill the interface conditions, so additional terms are required giving modified relations on
\Sigma
:

\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{S}}+\left(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}+\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}=m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{1},\quad-\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\mathrm{S}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{S}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{S}}=-\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}+m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{2}=\lambda,

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{S}}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}=m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{3},\quad-\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{S}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{f,j}=\mu_{f}\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}\sqrt{Z_{j}^{-1}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{f,j}+m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{4},\quad(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}})\cdot\tau_{f,j}=m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{5},

and the additional scalar and vector terms
m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{i}
(computed with the exact solutions (
8.1
) entail the following changes in the linear functionals

\displaystyle F(\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}})=\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{S}}\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}-\langle m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{4},\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\cdot\tau_{f,j}\rangle_{\Sigma},\qquad F(\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}):=\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\rho_{f}\phi\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{v}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}+\langle m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}\rangle_{\Sigma}+\langle m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{5},\boldsymbol{v}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\tau_{f,j}\rangle_{\Sigma},

\displaystyle F(\boldsymbol{w}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}):=\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}}\rho_{p}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{w}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}+\langle m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{3},\boldsymbol{w}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}+\langle m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{4},\boldsymbol{w}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\tau_{f,j}\rangle_{\Sigma}\rangle_{\Sigma},\qquad F(\mu):=-\langle m_{\Sigma,\mathrm{ex}}^{1},\mu\rangle_{\Sigma}.

We generate successively refined simplicial grids and use a sufficiently small (non dimensional) time step
\tau=h^{2}
and final time
T=1
, to guarantee that the error produced by the time discretization does not dominate. Errors between the approximate and exact solutions are shown in Table
1
. This error history confirms the optimal convergence of the FE scheme for all variables in their respective norms.

and

, using
\mathbb{P}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{1}-\mathbb{P}^{2}_{2}-\mathbb{P}^{1}-\mathbb{P}^{2}_{2}-\mathbb{P}_{1}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{1}
. The computations are performed at the last time step.

The backward Euler method is assessed for time convergence and verified by partitioning the time interval
(0,1)
into successively refined uniform discretizations and computing cumulative errors

\hat{e}_{s}=\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\tau\|s(t_{n+1})-s_{h}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{\star}\bigg)^{1/2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\star}
denotes the appropriate space norm for the generic vector or scalar field
s
. For this test we use a fixed mesh involving
923915
DoFs. The results are shown in Table
2
, confirming the expected first-order convergence.

8.2
Simulation of subsurface fracture flow

This test illustrates the applicability of the formulation in hydraulic fracturing and problem setup is similar to
[
3
,
38
]
. The physical units are meters for length, seconds for time, and KPa for pressure. Consider a rectangular domain
\Omega
with dimensions
(0,3.048)\times(0,6.096)
, which contains a relatively large fracture known as a macro void or open channel
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}
filled with an incompressible fluid, and the poro-hyperelastic domain is defined as
\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}=\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}
. The permeability
\kappa
and porosity
\phi
are heterogeneous but isotropic in the
xy
-plane, and derived from the non-smooth pattern found in the Society of Petroleum Engineers
\mathrm{SPE-10}
benchmark data/model 2
1
1

1

www.spe.org/web/csp
. We rescale this pattern as in
[
4
]
and map it onto a piecewise constant field using an unstructured triangular mesh for the poro-hyperelastic region. Note that the present formulation requires smooth porosity. Therefore, we project both the porosity and permeability data onto a
\mathbb{P}_{1}
field, as visualized in Figure
8.1
. There are 85 distinct layers within two general categories. We choose layer 80 from the dataset, which corresponds to the Upper Ness region exhibiting a fluvial fan pattern (flux channels of higher permeability and porosity). No gravity and no external loads are considered and the unstructured triangular mesh has
1629
elements for the Stokes region and
18897
elements for the poro-hyperelastic domain. We introduce a minor modification to the PDEs of the poroelastic region. Specifically, we incorporate
\mu_{f}\phi^{2}\kappa_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}
instead of
\phi^{2}\kappa_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{r}
.

The boundary conditions are chosen to be

\begin{array}[]{lll}\text{ Injection: }&\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{S}}=10,\quad\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{f}=0&\text{ on }\Sigma_{\mathrm{inflow}},\\
\text{ stress free: }&\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)=\boldsymbol{0},\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)=\boldsymbol{0}&\text{ on }\Sigma_{\mathrm{left}},\\
\text{ Normal relative velocity: }&\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}=0&\text{ on }\Sigma_{\mathrm{bottom}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{right}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{top}},\\
\text{ Normal displacement: }&\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}=0&\text{ on }\Sigma_{\mathrm{bottom}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{right}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{top}},\\
\text{ Shear traction: }&\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{f,j}=0,\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{f,j}=0&\text{ on }\Sigma_{\mathrm{bottom}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{right}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{top}}.\end{array}

The initial conditions are set accordingly to
\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{0})=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{0})=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\boldsymbol{u}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}(\boldsymbol{0})=\boldsymbol{0}\text{ and }p^{\mathrm{P}}(0)=0
. The total simulation time is
T=10\text{ hours}
and the time step is
\tau=30\mathrm{~s}
. The model parameters are taken as

\nu=0.2,\quad\mu_{f}=10^{-6},\quad\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}=1.0,\quad\rho_{f}=1000,\quad\rho_{p}=1016,\quad\theta=0,\quad c_{0}=6.89\times 10^{-2},\quad K=(1-\phi)^{2}/c_{0}

These parameters are realistic for hydraulic fracturing and are similar to the ones used in
[
20
,
33
]
.

The Lamé coefficients and bulk modulus are determined from the Young’s modulus
E
and the Poisson’s ratio
\nu
via the relationships
\lambda_{p}=E\nu/[(1+\nu)(1-2\nu)]\text{ and }\mu_{p}=E/[2(1+\nu)]
. Given the porosity
\phi
, the Young’s modulus is determined from the law
E(\boldsymbol{x})=10^{7}(1-2\phi(\boldsymbol{x}))^{2.1}
.

For this test, we use the Taylor–Hood
\mathbb{P}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{1}
elements for the fluid velocity and pressure in the fracture region, and
\mathbb{P}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{1}-\mathbb{P}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{1}^{2}
elements for the relative velocity, pressure, solid displacement and solid velocity in the porous medium, and continuous
\mathbb{P}_{1}
elements for the Lagrange multiplier. Snapshots of the approximate solutions (relative velocity, solid velocity, solid displacement, pressure in the poro-hyperelastic region, and fluid pressure and velocity magnitude in the Stokes region) for fluid injection into a fracture porous medium using the SPE10-based benchmark test are shown in Figure
8.2
. We observe that the most of the leak-off occurs through the fracture tip, where the relative velocity in the porous medium is greatest in a channel-like high-permeability region near the tip. The injected fluid causes an increase in pressure at the interface, and the anticipated channel-like progressive filtration from the Stokes domain to the poro-hyperelastic domain can be appreciated in the porous pressure plot on the left panel of Figure
8.2
, demonstrating higher values near the fracture tip.
We note that the model we are using allows for using a spatially varying porosity within the equation, which leads to a leakage near the base of the injection pointing downwards. A similar effect is seen at nearly two-thirds of the tip, with an upwards flow.

, permeability

, and Young modulus

) from layer 80 of the
\mathrm{SPE10}

dataset for reservoir simulations, herein projected onto a

field for the poro-hyperelastic sub-domain.

8.3
Channel filtration and stress build-up on interface deformation

We continue our set of numerical simulations addressing an important practical problem. We adopt a similar setup as in
[
38
]
. Consider the domains
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}=(-1,1)\times(0,2),\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}=(-1,1)\times(-2,0)
, separated by the interface
\Sigma=(-1,1)\times\{0\}
. Even if the present model is stated in the limit of small strains, it is possible to have large displacements, likely located near the interface (and without violating the model assumptions). In this scenario,

we smoothly move the fluid domain and the fluid mesh to avoid distortions generated near the interface. For this we use a standard harmonic extension that is solved at each time step: Find
\mathbf{d}_{h}^{*}=\mathbf{d}_{h}+\widehat{\mathbf{d}}_{h}
such that

-\Delta\widehat{\mathbf{d}}_{h}=\mathbf{0}\quad\text{ in }\Omega_{S},\quad\widehat{\mathbf{d}}_{h}=\mathbf{d}_{h}\text{ on }\Sigma,\quad\nabla\widehat{\mathbf{d}}_{h}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{S}=\boldsymbol{0}\text{ on walls of }\Omega_{S},\quad\text{ and }\quad\widehat{\mathbf{d}}_{h}=\mathbf{0}\text{ on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}the} inlet}.

Note that, in contrast to
[
38
]
, we are using a homogeneous Neumann boundary condition at the sides. Then
, we perform an
\mathbf{L}^{2}
-projection of both
\mathbf{d}_{h}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{d}}_{h}
into
\mathbf{V}_{s,h}+\mathbf{V}_{f,h}
and add them to obtain the global displacement
\mathbf{d}_{h}^{*}
. We enforce the effect of deformation on the Stokes domain by considering that
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}
is a deformed configuration, and solve in that domain the pulled-back equations according to the deformation
\varphi(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{x}+\widehat{\mathbf{d}}
.

We illustrate the effect of using harmonic extension by looking at the behavior of normal filtration into a 2D deformable porous medium. The boundary conditions is as follows, assuming that the flow is driven by pressure differences only. On the top segment we impose the fluid pressure
p^{\mathrm{S}}_{\text{in }}=2\sin^{2}(\pi t)
, and on the outlet (the bottom segment) the fluid pressure
p^{\mathrm{P}}_{\text{out }}=0
. On the vertical walls of
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}}
we set
\boldsymbol{u}_{f}^{\mathrm{S}}=\mathbf{0}
while on the vertical walls of
\Omega_{\mathrm{P}}
we set the slip conditions
\boldsymbol{y}_{s}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}=0
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{r}^{\mathrm{P}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{\mathrm{P}}=0
. The model parameters (all adimensional) are taken as

\kappa=0.005,\quad\lambda_{p}=10,\quad\mu_{p}=5,\quad\rho_{p}=1.07,\quad\rho_{f}=1,\quad\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}=0.1,\quad\mu_{f}=0.8,\quad\theta=0,\quad c_{0}=0.02,

\quad\phi=0.3,\quad K=(1-\phi)^{2}/c_{0}.

We assume that there are no body forces or gravity acting on the system. This example uses the same FEs as before and the numerical results are presented in Figure
8.3
. The effect of the interface is clearly seen in the poroelastic domain.
Close to the interface, the relative velocity, the solid displacement, and the fluid pressure are heterogeneous in the horizontal direction
before recovering the expected constant value (constant in the horizontal direction) expected in the far field. Also, we plot the overall displacement in the domain. From Figure
8.4
(see panels (b) and (c)) one can see that for large enough interfacial displacements, the elements close to it exhibit a large distortion.

,

, and

. Effect of using or not the harmonic extension to move the fluid domain (bottom and top, respectively).

8.4
Interfacial ﬂow in the brain

To conclude this section, we present a
2D
simulation related to brain biomechanics. More specifically,
we investigate how the incoming cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) flow from the spinal canal effects the brain tissues. In other words, we can say that the chosen problem is motivated by real-world applications like modeling the glymphatic system, where the brain porous tissue interacts with the surrounding CSF. For example, a heartbeat creates pressure waves in the CSF around the brain, which then spread through the brain.
A number of mechanical processes constantly affect the brain function: blood entering and leaving, fluid movements such as CSF and interstitial fluid in and around the brain and spine, pressures inside the skull, brain tissue shifts, and fluid flow between cells, for example.

Following
[
16
]
, we represent the brain parenchyma as a 2D poro-hyperelastic sub-domain
\Omega_{P}
, and the surrounding CSF-filled spaces as a free fluid (Stokes) sub-domain denoted by
\Omega_{S}
. These sub-domains share a common boundary
\Sigma=\Omega_{S}\cap\Omega_{P}
with normal vector
\boldsymbol{n}_{S}
, pointing from
\Omega_{S}
to
\Omega_{P}
on
\Sigma
and outwards on the boundary
\partial\Omega_{S}
. The following values for the material parameters are adopted from
[
16
,
23
,
44
]

\mu_{f}=7\times 10^{-7},\quad\alpha_{\mathrm{BJS}}=1,\quad c_{0}=2\times 10^{-5},\quad\phi=0.2,\quad K=(1-\phi)^{2}/c_{0},\quad\kappa_{f}=1\times 10^{-8},\quad\mu_{p}=267\times 10^{-3},

\lambda_{p}=26488\times 10^{-3},\quad\theta=0,\quad\rho_{f}=1.005,\quad\rho_{p}=1.03.

The traction boundary conditions are applied as follows: at the top right
(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{S}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{S}=(10,10))
and bottom right
(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{S}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{S}=(1,1))
regions in the axial slices of the brain, and at the top
(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{f}^{S}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{s}=(10,10))
region in the coronal slices of the brain. A homogeneous Dirichlet boundary condition is imposed on the remaining parts of the boundary. The snapshots of the approximate solutions (interstitial fluid pressure, interstitial fluid velocity, brain tissue displacement, brain tissue velocity, CSF pressure, CSF velocity) for the axial and coronal slices, which illustrate the interfacial flow in the brain, are shown in Figures
8.5
and
8.6
, respectively.

For the axial slices, the excess pore pressure that develops in the parenchyma drains through the entire interface. However, under the flow and loading rate regime being considered, the localization of Stokes fluid pressure and Stokes velocity (determined by boundary conditions on the bottom left) results in interfacial flow patterns where the pore pressure gradient is much higher near the bottom-left region with larger velocity. The Biot fluid pressure then dissipates throughout the remainder of the deformable porous domain, and the permeating patterns align with the brain displacement. As the flow eventually reaches the interface near the boundary (on the top right), the displacement is significantly smaller compared to the displacement near the center of the parenchyma.

In the coronal slices, we observe that both fluid pressure distributions in the subarachnoid space (region filled with CSF) and brain parenchyma exhibit a higher gradient near the portion of the interface closer to the boundary at the top. This results in a very smooth displacement field with a relatively higher (but still mild) magnitude near the top-center part of the interface. On average, the fluid pressure in the subarachnoid space remains higher than in the parenchyma. In both types of slices, the interstitial fluid (ISF) velocity within the brain parenchyma remains generally low relative to CSF flow. We note that, since the application here is in 2D, we refrain from drawing any conclusions regarding the phenomena in 3D.

. The traction boundary conditions at the top (coronal slices), respectively.

9
Conclusion

In this paper, we propose a model for the coupling between free fluid and a poro-hyperelastic body. This model is a novel contribution to the field of theoretical and numerical partial differential equations in interface coupled problems. We employed the Brinkman equation for fluid flow in the porous medium, incorporating inertial effects into the fluid dynamics. A Lagrange multiplier-based formulation is proposed and prove the existence and uniqueness of the continuous and discrete formulations. The proof of the existence relies on an auxiliary multi-valued parabolic problem. A priori error estimates for both the semi- and fully-discrete schemes are derived.
Theoretically, we observe that both relative velocity and solid displacement exhibit sub-optimal convergence. The solid and relative velocity blocks in the diagonal of the system matrix makes it difficult to derive a bound in the energy norm.
We conducted numerical validation of spatio-temporal accuracy. The numerical convergence tests show a suboptimal convergence for relative velocity and optimal convergence for solid displacement in their respective norms. Additionally, we observe superconvergence for Stokes pressure. We also performed tests of applicative relevance, studying the behavior of poromechanical filtration in subsurface hydraulic fracture with challenging heterogeneous material parameters and channel filtration when stress builds up on interface deformation. The set of tests also includes a typical application in biomechanical modeling of the brain to study how the incoming cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) flow from the spinal canal effects the brain tissues. These results can contribute in the prediction of important mechanisms in the overall brain function. Further perspectives of this work include the extension to the fully nonlinear regime, as well as other type of transmission conditions that would allow more generality in the type of poromechanical problems we can tackle. While the model and the continuous and discrete analyses are valid also in the 3D case, our numerical examples were only in 2D, due to the substantial increase in computational and memory requirements. Also, the interface in 3D brain geometries is significantly larger and more complex than in 2D cases, increasing the number of degrees of freedom at the interface. We therefore foresee the development of suitable parameter-robust block preconditioners.
